Based on the search results, I found information about SmolVLA LIBERO evaluation with the `eval_libero` code and success rates:

## Key Findings

**SmolVLA Performance on LIBERO Benchmark:**

Recent evaluation results show success rates of 93.0% on libero_object, 81.0% on libero_goal, 63.0% on libero_spatial, and 56.0% on libero_10, with an overall 73.25% success rate (as of April 2026).

**Evaluation Code and Process:**

The evaluation command for LIBERO uses `lerobot-eval` with parameters like `--env.type=libero`, `--env.task` to pick specific suites (libero_object, libero_spatial, etc.), `--eval.batch_size` to control parallel environments, and `--eval.n_episodes` to set the total number of episodes to run.

**Context on Reproducibility:**

There are documented challenges with reproducing published results. Some users report that when evaluating the official SmolVLA LIBERO model, performance improves from 0.43 to 0.51 on libero_10 when evaluated solely, but still remains below the original baseline.

**Documentation:**

LeRobot ported LIBERO into their framework and used it mainly to evaluate SmolVLA. LIBERO is now part of their multi-eval supported simulation, allowing benchmarking on single or multiple task suites.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

   out: dict[str, dict[int, Any]] = defaultdict(dict)\\n    for suite_name in suite_names:\\n        suite = _get_suite(suite_name)\\n        total = len(suite.tasks)\\n        selected = _select_task_ids(total, task_ids_filter)\\n        if not selected:\\n            raise ValueError(f\\\"No tasks selected for suite '{suite_name}' (available: {total}).\\\")\\n\\n        for tid in that all environments in the batch are run until the last environment is done. video_paths is list-like\\n        paths = metrics.get(\\\"video_paths\\\", [])\\n        if paths:\\n            group_acc[group][\\\"video_paths\\\"].extend(paths)\\n            overall[\\\"video_paths\\\"].extend(paths)\\n\\n    # Choose runner (sequential vs threaded)\\n    task_runner = partial(\\n        run_one,\\n        policy=policy,\\n        env_preprocessor=env_preprocessor,\\n        env_postprocessor=env_postprocessor,\\n        preprocessor=preprocessor,\\n        postprocessor=postprocessor,\\n        n_episodes=n_episodes,\\n        max_episodes_rendered=max_episodes_rendered,\\n        videos_dir=videos_dir,\\n        return_episode_data=return_episode_data,\\n        start_seed=start_seed,\\n    )\\n\\n    if max_parallel_tasks <= 1:\\n        # sequential path (single accumulator path on the main thread)\\n        # NOTE: keeping a single-threaded accumulator avoids concurrent list appends or locks\\n        for task_group, task_id, env in tasks:\\n            tg, tid, metrics = task_runner(task_group, task_id, env)\\n            _accumulate_to(tg, metrics)\\n            per_task_infos.append({\\\"task_group\\\": tg, \\\"task_id\\\": tid, \\\"metrics\\\": metrics})\\n    else:\\n        # threaded path: submit all tasks, consume completions on main thread and accumulate there\\n        with cf.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_parallel_tasks) as executor:\\n            fut2meta = {}\\n            for task_group, task_id, env in tasks:\\n                fut = executor.submit(task_runner, task_group, task_id, env)\\n                fut2meta[fut] = (task_group, task_id)\\n            for fut in cf.as_completed(fut2meta):\\n                tg, tid, metrics = fut.result()\\n                _accumulate_to(tg, metrics)\\n                per_task_infos.append({\\\"task_group\\\": tg, \\\"task_id\\\": tid, \\\"metrics\\\": metrics})\\n\\n    # compute aggregated metrics helper (robust to lists/scalars)\\n    def _agg_from_list(xs):\\n        if not xs:\\n            return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n        arr = np.array(xs, dtype=float)\\n        return float(np.nanmean(arr))\\n\\n    # compute per-group aggregates\\n    groups_aggregated = {}\\n    for group, acc in group_acc.items():\\n        groups_aggregated[group] = {\\n            \\\"avg_sum_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(acc[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n            \\\"avg_max_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(acc[\\\"max_rewards\\\"]),\\n            \\\"pc_success\\\": _agg_from_list(acc[\\\"successes\\\"]) * 100 if acc[\\\"successes\\\"] else float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n            \\\"n_episodes\\\": len(acc[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n            \\\"video_paths\\\": list(acc[\\\"video_paths\\\"]),\\n        }\\n\\n    # overall aggregates\\n    overall_agg = {\\n        \\\"avg_sum_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"avg_max_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"max_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"pc_success\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"successes\\\"]) * 100 if overall[\\\"successes\\\"] else float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n        \\\"n_episodes\\\": len(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"eval_s\\\": time.time() - start_t,\\n        \\\"eval_ep_s\\\": (time.time() - start_t) / max(1, len(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"])),\\n        \\\"video_paths\\\": list(overall[\\\"video_paths\\\"]),\\n    }\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"per_task\\\": per_task_infos,\\n        \\\"per_group\\\": groups_aggregated,\\n        \\\"overall\\\": overall_agg,\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    init_logging()\\n    register_third_party_plugins()\\n    eval_main()\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.83}}"

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a very clear picture. Let me analyze:

1. The project has config references to `eval_success_rate_libero.py` in the config comments, but this file doesn't exist!
2. The existing eval scripts (`eval_sim.py`, `eval_success_rate.py`) are for aloha_sim_insertion, NOT LIBERO.
3. The official lerobot has `lerobot/envs/libero.py` with proper LIBERO env wrapping and `lerobot/scripts/lerobot_eval.py` for evaluation.

Key observations from the official lerobot LIBERO code:


- Task suite max steps vary by type: spatial and object tasks use 280 steps, goal tasks use 300, libero_10 uses 520, and libero_90 uses 400
- The action space is 7-dimensional
- The environment captures two camera views: agentview_image and robot0_eye_in_hand_image
- Control operates in relative mode with 256x256 image observations
- Success is determined when the LIBERO environment returns `terminated=True` from its internal `_check_success()` method
- Initial states come from the LIBERO benchmark's predefined configurations

Now I'm looking at the evaluation pipeline in lerobot's `lerobot_eval.py` to understand how it orchestrates the evaluation process.

The `rollout()` function handles the core evaluation loop, and the policy itself manages action chunking rather than the eval script doing it. Success detection relies on the environment's `_check_success()` method. For SmolVLA specifically, I need to understand how it performs action inference using flow matching, how many denoising steps it uses, how action chunking is configured, what image preprocessing it applies, and how it unnormalizes actions. Let me dig into the SmolVLA policy implementation in lerobot.
```

(M,)\\n            axis = quat[mask, :3] / den[mask].unsqueeze(1)\\n            result[mask] = axis * angle.unsqueeze(1)\\n\\n        return result\\n\\n\\n@dataclass\\n@ProcessorStepRegistry.register(name=\\\"isaaclab_arena_processor\\\")\\nclass IsaaclabArenaProcessorStep(ObservationProcessorStep):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Processes IsaacLab Arena observations into LeRobot format.\\n\\n    **State Processing:**\\n    - Extracts state components from obs[\\\"policy\\\"] based on `state_keys`.\\n    - Concatenates into a flat vector mapped to \\\"observation.state\\\".\\n\\n    **Image Processing:**\\n    - Extracts images from obs[\\\"camera_obs\\\"] based on `camera_keys`.\\n    - Converts from (B, H, W, C) uint8 to (B, C, H, W) float32 [0, 1].\\n    - Maps to \\\"observation.images.&lt;camera_name>\\\".\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    # Configurable from IsaacLabEnv config / cli args: --env.state_keys=\\\"robot_joint_pos,left_eef_pos\\\"\\n    state_keys: tuple[str, ...]\\n\\n    # Configurable from IsaacLabEnv config / cli args: --env.camera_keys=\\\"robot_pov_cam_rgb\\\"\\n    camera_keys: tuple[str, ...]\\n\\n    def _process_observation(self, observation):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        Processes both image and policy state observations from IsaacLab Arena.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        processed_obs = {}\\n\\n        if f\\\"{OBS_STR}.camera_obs\\\" in observation:\\n            camera_obs = observation[f\\\"{OBS_STR}.camera_obs\\\"]\\n\\n            for cam_name, img in camera_obs.items():\\n                if cam_name not in self.camera_keys:\\n                    continue\\n\\n                img = img.permute(0, 3, 1, 2).contiguous()\\n                if img.dtype == torch.uint8:\\n                    img = img.float() / 255.0\\n                elif img.dtype != torch.float32:\\n                    img = img.float()\\n\\n                processed_obs[f\\\"{OBS_IMAGES}.{cam_name}\\\"] = img\\n\\n        # Flatten extra dims: (B, N, M) -> (B, N*M)\\n                    if component.dim() > 2:\\n                        batch_size = component.shape[0]\\n                        component = component.view(batch_size, -1)\\n                    state_components.append(component)\\n\\n            if state_components:\\n                state = torch.cat(state_components, dim=-1)\\n                state = state.float()\\n                processed_obs[OBS_STATE] = state\\n\\n        return processed_obs\\n\\n    def transform_features(\\n        self, features: dict[PipelineFeatureType, dict[str, PolicyFeature]]\\n    ) -> dict[PipelineFeatureType, dict[str, PolicyFeature]]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Not used for policy evaluation.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        return features\\n\\n    def observation(self, observation):\\n        return self._process_observation(observation)\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"}

Key findings so far:

SmolVLA Configuration:
- `chunk_size: int = 50` (action chunk size)
- `n_action_steps: int = 50` (actions to execute per chunk)
- `num_steps: int = 10` (denoising steps for flow matching)
- `resize_imgs_with_padding: tuple[int, int] = (512, 512)` (image resize)
- `tokenizer_max_length: int = 48`
- Normalization: STATE → MEAN_STD, ACTION → MEAN_STD, VISUAL → IDENTITY


- LIBERO uses 256x256 images with two camera views (agentview and in-hand), operates in relative control mode with action dimension of 7, and applies a 180° rotation to images during preprocessing while encoding end-effector position, orientation, and gripper state as the observation.

Now I'm checking whether the project actually has the evaluation script it references, and I need to trace through how SmolVLA's action sampling works during inference to understand the complete pipeline.

torch.cumsum(prefix_pad_masks, dim=1) - 1\\n        # Compute image and language key value cache\\n        _, past_key_values = self.vlm_with_expert.forward(\\n            attention_mask=prefix_att_2d_masks,\\n            position_ids=prefix_position_ids,\\n            past_key_values=None,\\n            inputs_embeds=[prefix_embs, None],\\n            use_cache=self.config.use_cache,\\n            fill_kv_cache=True,\\n        )\\n        num_steps = self.config.num_steps\\n        dt = -1.0 / num_steps\\n\\n        x_t = noise\\n        for step in range(num_steps):\\n            time = 1.0 + step * dt\\n            time_tensor = torch.tensor(time, dtype=torch.float32, device=device).expand(bsize)\\n\\n            def denoise_step_partial_call(input_x_t, current_timestep=time_tensor):\\n                return self.denoise_step(\\n                    x_t=input_x_t,\\n                    prefix_pad_masks=prefix_pad_masks,\\n                    past_key_values=past_key_values,\\n                    timestep=current_timestep,\\n                )\\n\\n            if self._rtc_enabled():\\n                inference_delay = kwargs.get(\\\"inference_delay\\\")\\n                prev_chunk_left_over = kwargs.get(\\\"prev_chunk_left_over\\\")\\n                execution_horizon = kwargs.get(\\\"execution_horizon\\\")\\n\\n                v_t = self.rtc_processor.denoise_step(\\n                    x_t=x_t,\\n                    prev_chunk_left_over=prev_chunk_left_over,\\n                    inference_delay=inference_delay,\\n                    time=time,\\n                    original_denoise_step_partial=denoise_step_partial_call,\\n                    execution_horizon=execution_horizon,\\n                )\\n            else:\\n                v_t = denoise_step_partial_call(x_t)\\n\\n            x_t = x_t + dt * v_t\\n\\n            if self.rtc_processor is not None and self.rtc_processor.is_debug_enabled():\\n                self.rtc_processor.track(time=time, x_t=x_t, v_t=v_t)\\n\\n        return x_t\\n\\n    def denoise_step(\\n        self,\\n        prefix_pad_masks,\\n        past_key_values,\\n        x_t,\\n        timestep,\\n    ):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply one denoising step of the noise `x_t` at a given timestep.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        suffix_embs, suffix_pad_masks, suffix_att_masks = self.embed_suffix(x_t, timestep)\\n\\n        suffix_len = suffix_pad_masks.shape[1]\\n        batch_size = prefix_pad_masks.shape[0]\\n        prefix_len = prefix_pad_masks.shape[1]\\n        prefix_pad_2d_masks = prefix_pad_masks[:, None, :].expand(batch_size, suffix_len, prefix_len)\\n\\n        suffix_att_2d_masks = make_att_2d_masks(suffix_pad_masks, suffix_att_masks)\\n\\n        full_att_2d_masks = torch.cat([prefix_pad_2d_masks, suffix_att_2d_masks], dim=2)\\n        prefix_offsets = torch.sum(prefix_pad_masks, dim=-1)[:, None]\\n        position_ids = prefix_offsets + torch.cumsum(suffix_pad_masks, dim=1) - 1\\n\\n        outputs_embeds, _ = self.vlm_with_expert.forward(\\n            attention_mask=full_att_2d_masks,\\n            position_ids=position_ids,\\n            past_key_values=past_key_values,\\n            inputs_embeds=[None, suffix_embs],\\n            use_cache=self.config.use_cache,\\n            fill_kv_cache=False,\\n        )\\n        suffix_out = outputs_embeds[1]\\n        suffix_out = suffix_out[:, -self.config.chunk_size :]\\n        suffix_out = suffix_out.to(dtype=torch.float32)\\n        v_t = self.action_out_proj(suffix_out)\\n        return v_t\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

SmolVLA is composed of a pretrained VLM,\\nand an action expert.\\n```bash\\nlerobot-train \\\\\\n--policy.type=smolvla \\\\\\n--dataset.repo_id=&lt;USER>/svla_so100_task1_v3 \\\\\\n--batch_size=64 \\\\\\n--steps=200000\\n```\\n\\nExample of using the smolvla pretrained model outside LeRobot training framework:\\n```python\\npolicy = SmolVLAPolicy.from_pretrained(\\\"lerobot/smolvla_base\\\")\\n```\\n\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport math\\nfrom collections import deque\\nfrom typing import TypedDict, Unpack\\n\\nimport torch\\nimport torch.nn.functional as F  # noqa: N812\\nfrom torch import Tensor, nn\\n\\nfrom lerobot.policies.pretrained import PreTrainedPolicy\\nfrom lerobot.policies.rtc.modeling_rtc import RTCProcessor\\nfrom lerobot.policies.smolvla.configuration_smolvla import SmolVLAConfig\\nfrom lerobot.policies.smolvla.smolvlm_with_expert import SmolVLMWithExpertModel\\nfrom lerobot.policies.utils import (\\n    populate_queues,\\n)\\nfrom lerobot.utils.constants import ACTION, OBS_LANGUAGE_ATTENTION_MASK, OBS_LANGUAGE_TOKENS, OBS_STATE\\nfrom lerobot.utils.device_utils import get_safe_dtype\\n\\n\\nclass ActionSelectKwargs(TypedDict, total=False):\\n    inference_delay: int | None\\n    prev_chunk_left_over: Tensor | None\\n    execution_horizon: int | None\\n\\n\\ndef create_sinusoidal_pos_embedding(\\n    time: torch.tensor, dimension: int, min_period: float, max_period: float, device=\\\"cpu\\\"\\n) -> Tensor:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Computes sine-cosine positional embedding vectors for scalar positions.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if dimension % 2 != 0:\\n        raise ValueError(f\\\"dimension ({dimension}) must be divisible by 2\\\")\\n\\n    if time.ndim != 1:\\n        raise ValueError(\\\"The time tensor is expected to be of shape `(batch_size, )`.\\\")\\n\\n    dtype = get_safe_dtype(torch.float64, device.type)\\n    fraction = torch.linspace(0.0, 1.0, dimension // 2, dtype=dtype, device=device)\\n    period = min_period * (max_period / min_period) ** fraction\\n\\n    # Compute the outer product\\n    scaling_factor = 1.0 / period * 2 * math.pi\\n    sin_input = scaling_factor[None, :] * time[:, None]\\n    pos_emb = torch.cat([torch.sin(sin_input), torch.cos(sin_input)], dim=1)\\n    return pos_emb\\n\\n\\ndef make_att_2d_masks(pad_masks, att_masks):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Copied from big_vision.\\n\\n    Tokens can attend to valid inputs tokens which have a cumulative mask_ar\\n    smaller or equal to theirs. This way `mask_ar` int[B, N] can be used to\\n    setup several types of attention, for example:\\n\\n      [[1 1 1 1 1 1]]: pure causal attention.\\n\\n      [[0 0 0 1 1 1]]: prefix-lm attention. The first 3 tokens can attend between\\n          themselves and the last 3 tokens have a causal attention. Now I have a very comprehensive understanding. Let me also check the rollout function in lerobot_eval.py to understand how the official eval pipeline works with action chunking. But I already see that:

1. `select_action` manages an action queue internally. It calls `_get_action_chunk` when the queue is empty, gets chunk_size (50) actions, takes n_action_steps (50) of them, and executes them one at a time.

2. The flow matching uses:
   - `num_steps = 10` denoising steps
   - Euler ODE solver: `x_t = x_t + dt * v_t` where `dt = -1/num_steps`
   - Time progresses from 1.0 to 0.0 as noise gets cleaned up

Looking at the rollout function in lerobot_eval.py, it follows a straightforward loop: preprocess the observation, call the policy's select_action method, postprocess the resulting action, step the environment, and check if we're done—all while managing environment resets with seeds.

Now I'm examining how the LIBERO environment handles actions and success detection. The step method scales actions from the policy's [-1, 1] output into the robot's native control space, treating them as relative deltas for movement, and uses `_check_success()` to determine task completion. I also see the observations are 256x256 images. Let me look more carefully at the step implementation to understand the full flow.

 task_ids_filter = gym_kwargs.pop(\\\"task_ids\\\", None)  # optional: limit to specific tasks\\n\\n    camera_names = _parse_camera_names(camera_name)\\n    suite_names = [s.strip() for s in \\\"observation.language.tokens\\\": lang_tokens,\\n        \\\"observation.language.attention_mask\\\": lang_mask,\\n    }\\n\\n\\n# vis_tokens.shape[0]\\n    noise = bm.sample_noise(\\n        (B, bm.config.chunk_size, bm.config.max_action_dim), device\\n    )\\n    time = torch.ones(B, device=device)\\n    suffix_embs, _, _ = bm.embed_suffix(noise, time)\\n\\n    scores = wrapper.acis(suffix_embs, vis_tokens)\\n\\n    N_vis = vis_tokens.shape[1]\\n    k = max(1, int(N_vis * wrapper.k_ratio))\\n    _, topk_idx = torch.topk(scores, k, dim=-1)\\n    mask = torch.zeros(B, N_vis, device=device)\\n    mask.scatter_(1, topk_idx, 1.0)\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef register_acis_hook(connector, mask:         return output\\n        masked = tokens * mask[: tokens.shape[0]].unsqueeze(-1)\\n        if isinstance(output, tuple):\\n            return (masked,) + output[1:]\\n        return masked\\n\\n    return connector.register_forward_hook(hook_fn)\\n\\n\\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n# Action prediction via flow matching ODE vis_tokens.shape[0]\\n    noise = bm.sample_noise(\\n        (B, bm.config.chunk_size, bm.config.max_action_dim), device\\n    )\\n    time = torch.ones(B, device=device)\\n    suffix_embs, _, _ = torch.topk(scores, k, dim=-1)\\n    mask = torch.zeros(B, N_vis, device=device)\\n    mask.scatter_(1, topk_idx, 1.0)\\n    return mask\\n\\n\\ndef register_acis_hook(connector, mask: Renaming features to match pretrained configurations.\\n    2.  Normalizing input and output features based on dataset statistics.\\n    3.  Adding a batch dimension.\\n    4.  Ensuring the language task description ends with a newline character.\\n    5.  Tokenizing the language task description.\\n    6.  Moving all data to the specified device.\\n\\n    The post-processing pipeline handles the model's output by:\\n    1.  Moving data to the CPU.\\n    2.  Unnormalizing the output actions to their original scale.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        config: The configuration object for the SmolVLA policy.\\n        dataset_stats: A dictionary of statistics for normalization.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        A tuple containing the configured pre-processor and post-processor pipelines.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    input_steps = [\\n        RenameObservationsProcessorStep(rename_map={}),  # To mimic the same processor as pretrained one\\n        AddBatchDimensionProcessorStep(),\\n        SmolVLANewLineProcessor(),\\n        TokenizerProcessorStep(\\n            tokenizer_name=config.vlm_model_name,\\n            padding=config.pad_language_to,\\n            padding_side=\\\"right\\\",\\n            max_length=config.tokenizer_max_length,\\n        ),\\n        DeviceProcessorStep(device=config.device),\\n        NormalizerProcessorStep(\\n            features={**config.input_features, **config.output_features},\\n            norm_map=config.normalization_mapping,\\n            stats=dataset_stats,\\n        ),\\n    ]\\n    output_steps = [\\n        UnnormalizerProcessorStep(\\n            features=config.output_features, norm_map=config.normalization_mapping, stats=dataset_stats\\n        ),\\n        DeviceProcessorStep(device=\\\"cpu\\\"),\\n    ]\\n    return (\\n        PolicyProcessorPipeline[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]](\\n            steps=input_steps,\\n            name=POLICY_PREPROCESSOR_DEFAULT_NAME,\\n        ),\\n        PolicyProcessorPipeline[PolicyAction, PolicyAction](\\n            steps=output_steps,\\n            name=POLICY_POSTPROCESSOR_DEFAULT_NAME,\\n            to_transition=policy_action_to_transition,\\n            to_output=transition_to_policy_action,\\n        ),\\n    )\\n\\n\\n@ProcessorStepRegistry.register(name=\\\"smolvla_new_line_processor\\\")\\nclass SmolVLANewLineProcessor(ComplementaryDataProcessorStep):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    A processor step that ensures the 'task' description ends with a newline character.\\n\\n    This step is necessary for certain tokenizers (e.g., PaliGemma) that expect a\\n    newline at the end of the prompt. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"def rollout(\\n    env: gym.vector.VectorEnv,\\n    policy: PreTrainedPolicy,\\n    env_preprocessor: PolicyProcessorPipeline[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]],\\n    env_postprocessor: PolicyProcessorPipeline[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]],\\n    preprocessor: PolicyProcessorPipeline[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]],\\n    postprocessor: PolicyProcessorPipeline[PolicyAction, PolicyAction],\\n    seeds: list[int] | None = None,\\n    return_observations: bool = False,\\n    render_callback: Callable[[gym.vector.VectorEnv], None] | None = None,\\n) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run a batched policy rollout once through a batch of environments.\\n\\n    Note that all environments in the batch are run until the last environment is done. This means some\\n    data will probably need to be discarded (for environments that aren't the first one to be done).\\n\\n    The return dictionary contains:\\n        (optional) \\\"observation\\\": A dictionary of (batch, sequence + 1, *) tensors mapped to observation\\n            keys. NOTE that this has an extra sequence element relative to the other keys in the\\n            dictionary. This is because an extra observation is included for after the environment is\\n            terminated or truncated.\\n        \\\"action\\\": A (batch, sequence, action_dim) tensor of actions applied based on the observations (not\\n            including the last observations).\\n        \\\"reward\\\": A (batch, sequence) tensor of rewards received for applying the actions.\\n        \\\"success\\\": A (batch, sequence) tensor of success conditions (the only time this can be True is upon\\n            environment termination/truncation).\\n        \\\"done\\\": A (batch, sequence) tensor of **cumulative** done conditions. For any given batch element,\\n            the first True is followed by True's all the way till the end. This can be used for masking\\n            extraneous elements from the sequences above.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        env: The batch of environments.\\n        policy: The policy. Must be a PyTorch nn module.\\n        seeds: The environments are seeded once at the start of the rollout. If provided, this argument\\n            specifies the seeds for each of the environments.\\n        return_observations: Whether to include all observations in the returned rollout data. Observations\\n            are returned optionally because they typically take more memory to cache. Defaults to False.\\n        render_callback: Optional rendering callback to be used after the environments are reset, and after\\n            every step.\\n    Returns:\\n        The dictionary described above.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    assert isinstance(policy, nn.Module), \\\"Policy must be a PyTorch nn module.\\\"\\n\\n    # Reset the policy and environments.\\n    policy.reset()\\n    observation, info = env.reset(seed=seeds)\\n    if render_callback is not None:\\n        render_callback(env)\\n\\n    all_observations = []\\n    all_actions = []\\n    all_rewards = []\\n    all_successes = []\\n    all_dones = []\\n\\n    step = 0\\n    # Keep track of which environments are done.\\n    done = np.array([False] * env.num_envs)\\n    max_steps = env.call(\\\"_max_episode_steps\\\")[0]\\n    progbar = trange(\\n        max_steps,\\n        desc=f\\\"Running rollout with at most {max_steps} steps\\\",\\n        disable=inside_slurm(),  # we dont want progress bar when we use slurm, since it clutters the logs\\n        leave=False,\\n    )\\n    check_env_attributes_and_types(env)\\n    while not np.all(done) and step < max_steps:\\n        # Numpy array to tensor and changing dictionary keys to LeRobot policy format.\\n        observation = preprocess_observation(observation)\\n        if return_observations:\\n            all_observations.append(deepcopy(observation))\\n\\n        # Infer \\\"task\\\" from attributes of environments.\\n        # TODO: works with SyncVectorEnv but not AsyncVectorEnv\\n        observation = add_envs_task(env, observation)\\n\\n        # Apply environment-specific preprocessing (e.g., LiberoProcessorStep for LIBERO)\\n        observation = env_preprocessor(observation)\\n\\n        observation = preprocessor(observation)\\n\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 21128 字符] ...\\n\\nected to contain 'sum_rewards', 'max_rewards', 'successes', optionally 'video_paths'\\n        # but eval_one may store per-episode lists; we assume metrics uses scalars averaged per task as before.\\n        # To be robust, accept scalars or lists.\\n        def _append(key, value):\\n            if value is None:\\n                return\\n            if isinstance(value, list):\\n                group_acc[group][key].extend(value)\\n                overall[key].extend(value)\\n            else:\\n                group_acc[group][key].append(value)\\n                overall[key].append(value)\\n\\n        _append(\\\"sum_rewards\\\", metrics.get(\\\"sum_rewards\\\"))\\n        _append(\\\"max_rewards\\\", metrics.get(\\\"max_rewards\\\"))\\n        _append(\\\"successes\\\", metrics.get(\\\"successes\\\"))\\n        # video_paths is list-like\\n        paths = metrics.get(\\\"video_paths\\\", [])\\n        if paths:\\n            group_acc[group][\\\"video_paths\\\"].extend(paths)\\n            overall[\\\"video_paths\\\"].extend(paths)\\n\\n    # Choose runner (sequential vs threaded)\\n    task_runner = partial(\\n        run_one,\\n        policy=policy,\\n        env_preprocessor=env_preprocessor,\\n        env_postprocessor=env_postprocessor,\\n        preprocessor=preprocessor,\\n        postprocessor=postprocessor,\\n        n_episodes=n_episodes,\\n        max_episodes_rendered=max_episodes_rendered,\\n        videos_dir=videos_dir,\\n        return_episode_data=return_episode_data,\\n        start_seed=start_seed,\\n    )\\n\\n    if max_parallel_tasks <= 1:\\n        # sequential path (single accumulator path on the main thread)\\n        # NOTE: keeping a single-threaded accumulator avoids concurrent list appends or locks\\n        for task_group, task_id, env in tasks:\\n            tg, tid, metrics = task_runner(task_group, task_id, env)\\n            _accumulate_to(tg, metrics)\\n            per_task_infos.append({\\\"task_group\\\": tg, \\\"task_id\\\": tid, \\\"metrics\\\": metrics})\\n    else:\\n        # threaded path: submit all tasks, consume completions on main thread and accumulate there\\n        with cf.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_parallel_tasks) as executor:\\n            fut2meta = {}\\n            for task_group, task_id, env in tasks:\\n                fut = executor.submit(task_runner, task_group, task_id, env)\\n                fut2meta[fut] = (task_group, task_id)\\n            for fut in cf.as_completed(fut2meta):\\n                tg, tid, metrics = fut.result()\\n                _accumulate_to(tg, metrics)\\n                per_task_infos.append({\\\"task_group\\\": tg, \\\"task_id\\\": tid, \\\"metrics\\\": metrics})\\n\\n    # compute aggregated metrics helper (robust to lists/scalars)\\n    def _agg_from_list(xs):\\n        if not xs:\\n            return float(\\\"nan\\\")\\n        arr = np.array(xs, dtype=float)\\n        return float(np.nanmean(arr))\\n\\n    # compute per-group aggregates\\n    groups_aggregated = {}\\n    for group, acc in group_acc.items():\\n        groups_aggregated[group] = {\\n            \\\"avg_sum_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(acc[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n            \\\"avg_max_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(acc[\\\"max_rewards\\\"]),\\n            \\\"pc_success\\\": _agg_from_list(acc[\\\"successes\\\"]) * 100 if acc[\\\"successes\\\"] else float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n            \\\"n_episodes\\\": len(acc[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n            \\\"video_paths\\\": list(acc[\\\"video_paths\\\"]),\\n        }\\n\\n    # overall aggregates\\n    overall_agg = {\\n        \\\"avg_sum_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"avg_max_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"max_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"pc_success\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"successes\\\"]) * 100 if overall[\\\"successes\\\"] else float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n        \\\"n_episodes\\\": len(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"eval_s\\\": time.time() - start_t,\\n        \\\"eval_ep_s\\\": (time.time() - start_t) / max(1, len(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"])),\\n        \\\"video_paths\\\": list(overall[\\\"video_paths\\\"]),\\n    }\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"per_task\\\": per_task_infos,\\n        \\\"per_group\\\": groups_aggregated,\\n        \\\"overall\\\": overall_agg,\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    init_logging()\\n    register_third_party_plugins()\\n    eval_main()\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}

overall aggregates\\n    overall_agg = {\\n        \\\"avg_sum_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"avg_max_reward\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"max_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"pc_success\\\": _agg_from_list(overall[\\\"successes\\\"]) * 100 if overall[\\\"successes\\\"] else float(\\\"nan\\\"),\\n        \\\"n_episodes\\\": len(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"]),\\n        \\\"eval_s\\\": time.time() - start_t,\\n        \\\"eval_ep_s\\\": (time.time() - start_t) / max(1, len(overall[\\\"sum_rewards\\\"])),\\n        \\\"video_paths\\\": list(overall[\\\"video_paths\\\"]),\\n    }\\n\\n    return {\\n        \\\"per_task\\\": per_task_infos,\\n        \\\"per_group\\\": groups_aggregated,\\n        \\\"overall\\\": overall_agg,\\n    }\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    init_logging()\\n    register_third_party_plugins()\\n    eval_main()\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == \\\"__main__\\\":\\n    main()\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"

Now I have a complete picture. Let me also check if LIBERO is installed on the server, and check the SmolVLA prepare_images / prepare_state methods in the modeling code.

Actually, let me also check if there's a normalization step for LIBERO. The official pipeline does:
1. LiberoProcessorStep: flip images 180°, convert robot_state to eef_pos(3)+axis_angle(3)+gripper_qpos(2)
2. SmolVLA preprocessor: normalize STATE with MEAN_STD, normalize ACTION with MEAN_STD


3. SmolVLA postprocessor: unnormalize output actions

This is the critical difference—the official pipeline normalizes and then unnormalizes, but our eval_success_rate.py (built for aloha, not LIBERO) skips this step entirely. I need to look at what SmolVLA's prepare_images and prepare_state methods actually do to understand the full preprocessing pipeline.
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"            loss = losses.mean()\\n            loss_dict[\\\"loss\\\"] = loss.item()\\n            return loss, loss_dict\\n\\n    def prepare_images(self, batch):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply SmolVLA preprocessing to the images, like resizing to 224x224 and padding to keep aspect ratio, and\\n        convert pixel range from [0.0, 1.0] to [-1.0, 1.0] as requested by SigLIP.\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        images = []\\n        img_masks = []\\n        present_img_keys = [key for key in self.config.image_features if key in batch]\\n        missing_img_keys = [key for key in self.config.image_features if key not in batch]\\n\\n        if len(present_img_keys) == 0:\\n            raise ValueError(\\n                f\\\"All image features are missing from the batch. timestep,\\n    ):\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Apply one denoising step of the noise `x_t` at a given timestep.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        suffix_embs, suffix_pad_masks, suffix_att_masks = self.embed_suffix(x_t, timestep)\\n\\n        suffix_len = suffix_pad_masks.shape[1]\\n        batch_size = prefix_pad_masks.shape[0]\\n        prefix_len = prefix_pad_masks.shape[1]\\n        prefix_pad_2d_masks = prefix_pad_masks[:, None, :].expand(batch_size, suffix_len, prefix_len)\\n\\n        suffix_att_2d_masks = make_att_2d_masks(suffix_pad_masks, suffix_att_masks)\\n\\n        full_att_2d_masks = torch.cat([prefix_pad_2d_masks, suffix_att_2d_masks], dim=2)\\n        prefix_offsets = torch.sum(prefix_pad_masks, dim=-1)[:, None]\\n        position_ids = prefix_offsets + torch.cumsum(suffix_pad_masks, dim=1) - 1\\n\\n        outputs_embeds, _ = self.vlm_with_expert.forward(\\n            attention_mask=full_att_2d_masks,\\n            position_ids=position_ids,\\n            past_key_values=past_key_values,\\n            inputs_embeds=[None, suffix_embs],\\n            use_cache=self.config.use_cache,\\n            fill_kv_cache=False,\\n        )\\n        suffix_out = outputs_embeds[1]\\n        suffix_out = suffix_out[:, -self.config.chunk_size :]\\n        suffix_out = suffix_out.to(dtype=torch.float32)\\n        v_t = self.action_out_proj(suffix_out)\\n        return v_t\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"
